\displaystyle F_{\mathcal{P}}(m)-G_{\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}(m)-\sum_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\triangleleft\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\ntrianglelefteq\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}G_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(m)-\sum_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\triangleleft\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}G_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(m)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{\mathcal{P}}(m)-G_{\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}(m)-\sum_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\triangleleft\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}G_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(m)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{\mathcal{P}}(m)-F_{\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}(m)\ ,

where we used the definition of
G_{\mathcal{P}}(m)
and that, by induction,
G_{P^{\prime}}(m)=0
for
P^{\prime}
such
P^{\prime}\triangleleft\mathcal{P}
and
P^{\prime}\ntrianglelefteq\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}
. Finally, since
Q_{2,0}=Q_{0,2}=P_{2}=P_{0}=0
, we deduce from (
79
) that
F_{\mathcal{P}}(m)=F_{\mathcal{P}^{(-1)}}(m)
so that
G_{\mathcal{P}}(m)=0
.

We now turn to the second result. Let us apply the generalized Möbius inversion formula for posets
[
35
, Sect.3.7]
to obtain an explicit expression for
G_{\mathcal{P}}(m)
:

G_{\mathcal{P}}(m)=\sum_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}}(-1)^{|\mathcal{P}|-|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|}F_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(m)\mu(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\ ,

(97)

where
\mu
is the Möbius function for partitions. More precisely, for
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}
where
\mathcal{P}
has
t
groups which respectively split into
s_{1}
,…,
s_{t}
block in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, we have
\mu(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})=(-1)^{|\mathcal{P}|-|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|}\prod_{r=1}^{t}(s_{r}-1)!
.

We denote
\kappa
the number of groups of
\mathcal{P}
of size larger to one and, without loss of generality, we denote these groups
\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}
. Writing
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}}
as a polynomial of
|\mathcal{P}|
variables, we then deduce from (
79
) that

\displaystyle G_{\mathcal{P}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{|\mathcal{P}|}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{l=\kappa+1}^{|\mathcal{P}|}x_{l}\right)\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{1}}\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{2}}\ldots\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\kappa}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}}(-1)^{t-\sum_{l=1}^{\kappa}|\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}|}}\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}(\underline{|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}|}-1)!

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}x_{l}^{|\mathcal{P}_{l}|}+\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+Q_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|,|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}(x_{l})\right]\right.

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left.\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|}P_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}(x_{l})\right]-\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[(x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}P_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|}(x_{l})\right]\right]\ .

where we consider any finer partition
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}
as a combination of
t
partitions of the groups
\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{t}
. We now prove that, for

r=1,\ldots,t
, the maximum degree of
x_{l}
in
G_{\mathcal{P}}
is at most
|\mathcal{P}_{r}|-2
and is at most
|\mathcal{P}_{r}|-3
when
\mathcal{P}_{l}\subset[2k]
or
\mathcal{P}_{r}\subset[2k+1,4k]
. Without loss of generality, we fix
r=1
. We only have to consider monomials with degree
|\mathcal{P}_{1}|
in
x_{1}
as there is no monomial of degree
|\mathcal{P}_{1}|-1
in
x_{1}
and no monomial of degree
|\mathcal{P}_{1}|-2
in
x_{1}
when
\mathcal{P}_{1}\subset[2k]
or
\mathcal{P}_{1}\subset[2k+1,4k]
. Gathering all the monomials in
G_{\mathcal{P}}
of degree
|\mathcal{P}_{1}|
in
x_{1}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle x_{1}^{|\mathcal{P}_{1}|}\left[\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{1}}(-1)^{1-|\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}|}(|\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}|-1)!\right]\left[\prod_{l=\kappa+1}^{|\mathcal{P}|}x_{l}\right]Z\ ;

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{2}}\ldots\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\kappa}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}}\prod_{l=2}^{\kappa}(-1)^{1-|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}|}(\underline{|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}|}-1)!\left[\prod_{l=2}^{\kappa}x_{l}^{|\mathcal{P}_{l}|}+\prod_{l=1}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+Q_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|,|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}(x_{l})\right]\right.

\displaystyle-\prod_{l=2}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|}P_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}(x_{l})\right]-\left.\prod_{l=2}^{\kappa}\prod_{\mathcal{Q}\in\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{l}}}\left[(x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}|}+x_{l}^{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k+1:4k]|}P_{|\mathcal{Q}\cap[2k]|}(x_{l})\right]\right]\ .

Finally, we need to compute
M_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}:=\sum_{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}\trianglelefteq\mathcal{P}_{1}}(-1)^{1-|\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}|}(|\underline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}}|-1)!
. Applying again the generalized inverse Möbius inverse formula for posets with the constant functional
A_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}=1
for any partition
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
, we conclude that
M_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}=0
. As a consequence, the degree of
G_{P}
in
x_{1}
is at most
|\mathcal{P}_{1}|-2
and is at most
\mathcal{P}_{1}-3
if
\mathcal{P}_{1}\subset[2k]
or
\mathcal{P}_{1}\subset[2k+1,4k]
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
9.3
.

It follows from (
) that
G_{\mathcal{P}}
decomposes into a sum of at most
(4k)^{4k}
– upper bound for the number of sub-partitions– sum of four polynomials (see the two last lines in (
)). Each of these four polynomial decomposes into at most
2^{2k}
monomials. Finally, it follows from Lemma
9.1
that the coefficient associated to each of these monomials is smaller or equal
(4k-1)!(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}})^{4k}(4k)^{4k+2}
.

\sum_{D\in\mathrm{Deg}(\mathcal{P})}|\alpha_{D,\mathcal{P}}|\leq 4\cdot 2^{2k}(4k)^{12k+2}(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}})^{4k}\leq(c^{\prime}\|E\|_{\psi_{2}})^{4k}k^{12k+2}\kern 5.0pt.

∎

9.7
Proof of Corollary
6.4

Proof of Corollary
6.4
.

The proof follows the same steps as for Corollary
3.4
. First we observe that
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}
so that

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k}\right)^{2}\right]\leq(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}k)^{c^{\prime}k}\left[(pq)^{k}+pq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{4k-4}+q\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{4k-2}\right]

We consider two cases depending on
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}
. If
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}\leq(pq)^{1/4}
, then the above risk bound simplifies in

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|(U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k})^{2}\right]\leq 3(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}k)^{c^{\prime}k}(pq)^{k}
. Then, together with the inequality
|x^{1/(2k)}-y^{1/(2k)}|\leq|x-y|^{1/(2k)}
and Hölder’s inequality, we arrive at

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|(U_{k})_{+}^{1/(2k)}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}|\right]\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2k}_{2k}|\right]^{1/2k}\leq c^{\prime\prime}(\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}k)^{c_{3}}(pq)^{1/4}\kern 5.0pt.

Turning to the case where
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}\geq(pq)^{1/4}
,
we use
|(1+x)^{1/(2k)}-1|\leq 2|x|
for any
x\geq-1/2
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|(U_{k})_{+}^{1/(2k)}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}\big|[U_{k}/\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2k}_{2k}]^{\frac{1}{2k}}-1\big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[U_{k}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k}/2\right]+2\|\mathbf{A}\|^{1-2k}_{2k}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}\left[4\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[(U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k})^{2}\right]}{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4k}_{2k}}+2\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[(U_{k}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2k})^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2k}_{2k}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}k)^{c^{\prime}k}\left[\frac{(pq)^{k}}{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4k-1}_{2k}}+\frac{(pq)^{k/2}}{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2k-1}_{2k}}+\frac{pq+\sqrt{pq}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}}{\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}^{2}}+\frac{q}{\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2k}}+\sqrt{q}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}k)^{c^{\prime}k}(pq)^{1/4}\ .

The result follows.
∎

9.8
Proofs of Propositions
6.1
and
6.2

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

As in proof of Proposition
2.5
, we start from (
36
)

\big|\|\mathbf{Y}^{T}\mathbf{Y}\|_{\infty}-p-\|\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}\big|\leq 2\|\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}\|{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt.

We control
\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}\|_{\infty}
using (
100
) and
\|{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}
as in Lemma
9.15
. The remainder of the proof is as in the proof of Proposition
2.5
and we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[|(\|\mathbf{Y}^{T}\mathbf{Y}\|_{\infty}-p)_{+}^{1/2}-\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}|\right]\leq c_{\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}}(pq)^{1/4}\ .

∎

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

We argue as in the proof of Proposition
4.3
. Starting from (
), we have

\displaystyle|T^{s}_{s}-\|\mathbf{A}\|^{s}_{s}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{s/2}s\sum_{i=1}^{q}\left[\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|^{2}_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}_{q}\|_{\infty}+2\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{A})\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}\right]\sigma^{s-2}_{i}(\mathbf{A})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+3^{s/2-1}\left[\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|^{s}_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}_{q}\|^{s/2}_{\infty}+2^{s/2}\sigma^{s/2}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|^{s/2}_{\infty}\right]\ ,

and we only have to control moments of
\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}
and
\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}_{q}\|_{\infty}
.

Lemma 9.15
.

There exists a numerical constant
c>0
, such that for any
t>0
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}\leq c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}\left[\sqrt{q}+\sqrt{t}\right]\right]\geq 1-e^{-t}\ .

(99)

Next, we turn to the control of
\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}_{q}\|_{\infty}
which is a straightforward application of Theorem 9 in
[
20
]
. There exists a constant
c_{\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}}
that only depends on
\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}
such that for any
t
,

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-p\mathbf{I}_{q}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}}\left[\sqrt{pq}+q+\sqrt{pt}+t\right]\right]\geq 1-e^{-t}\ .

(100)

Integrating the deviation bounds of (
99
) and (
100
), we arrive as in (
67
) and (
69
)

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|T^{s}_{s}-\|\mathbf{A}\|^{s}_{s}|\right]\leq c^{s}_{\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}}\left[q[(p\vee s)(q\vee s)]^{s/4}+\sqrt{q}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{s-1}_{s-1}+{\mathbf{1}}_{s\geq 2}\sqrt{pq}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{s-2}^{s-2}\right]\ .

Then, the remainder of the proof is exactly as in the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9.15
.

We provide a simple but pedestrian proof based on
\epsilon
-nets. We start from

\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{u\in I(\mathbf{A}),|u|\leq 1}\quad\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{q}:\ |v|_{2}\leq 1}u^{T}\mathbf{E}v

(101)

Consider an
1/4
-net
\mathcal{N}_{1}
of the unit ball of
I(\mathbf{A})
and a
1/4
-net
\mathcal{N}_{2}
of the unit ball of
\mathbb{R}^{q}
.
Let
u^{*}
and
v^{*}
be any maximizer of the supremum in (
101
) and let
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
denote the corresponding closest points in
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(u^{*})^{T}\mathbf{E}v^{*}=(u^{\prime})^{T}\mathbf{E}v^{\prime}+(u^{*}-u^{\prime})^{T}\mathbf{E}v^{\prime}+(u^{*})^{T}\mathbf{E}(v^{*}-v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}u^{T}\mathbf{E}v+\frac{1}{2}\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}

so that

\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}u^{T}\mathbf{E}v

Since the mean zero random variable
u^{T}\mathbf{E}v
is sub-Gaussian with sub-Gaussian norm
\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}
, we deduce that, with probability higher than
1-e^{-t}
, one has

\|\Pi_{I(\mathbf{A})}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}\leq c\|E\|_{\psi_{2}}\left[\sqrt{t}+\sqrt{\log(|\mathcal{N}_{1}|)+\log(|\mathcal{N}_{2}|)}\right]\ ,

Finally, we conclude by observing that
\log(|\mathcal{N}_{2}|)+\log(|\mathcal{N}_{1}|)\leq c^{\prime}q
since the
\epsilon
-covering number of a
d
-dimensional unit ball is at most
d\log(3/\epsilon)
.
∎

A
Technical inequalities

For bounding the singular values of
\mathbf{E}
, we shall rely on the following results (taken e.g. from
[
11
]
).

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be a
p\times q
matrix whose entries are independent standard Gaussian random variables. Then,

\mathbb{E}\left[|\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{E})|\right]\leq\left(\sqrt{p}+\sqrt{q}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be a
p\times q
matrix whose entries are independent standard Gaussian random variables. Then, for any
t>0
, we have

\max\left\{\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{E}}\right)\geq\sqrt{p}+\sqrt{q}+\sqrt{2t}\right),\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{q}\left(\mathbf{E}\right)\leq\sqrt{p}-\sqrt{q}-\sqrt{2t}\right)\right\}\leq e^{-t}\ .

(102)

The following lemma is taken from
[
23
]

Lemma A.3
.

Let
Z
be distributed as
\chi^{2}(p)
random variable. For any
t>0
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}[Z\geq p+2\sqrt{pt}+2t]\leq e^{-t}\ .
